tbmm-2016-01-28sit01spe0008par0047	Mr. President, I have taken an off-the-book commitment to the constitutional process in Turkey. I salute your high council. The constitutional adventure of Turkish political history began with the Senedi Alliance and Tanzimat Fermani, and became official with the Law declared in 1876. From this date on, there have been extensive constitutional experiences in our country with the Constitutions of 1921, 1924, 1961 and 1982. Let's take a quick look at the constitutional process of our country. The 1921 Constitution, which reflects the basic institutional philosophy of the Republic of Turkey established in 1920, single-handedly collected legislation and governance in an effort to fight effectively in the Battle of Salvation. When the dates showed 1924, the country's conditions changed and Turkey met a new Constitution. The first and most important emphasis of this Constitution until 1961 is that the state's way of ruling is the republic. Although the 1924 Constitution envisions the parliamentary system of dominance, the only party system to continue until 1945 is the sole control of the party, rather than the rule of the nation's will. By May 27, 1960, the first intervention in Turkish democracy was seized, the Constitution was suspended, and the Constitution of 1961 was drafted by soldiers. From this period on, the soldier's political weight has increased, has become a habit of interfering with democracy, interfering with national will has been repeated almost every decade. As a matter of fact, in 1971, the government was given a notice, and in 1980, Turkish democracy was once again hit by military intervention, and the Armed Forces took over. The 1982 Constitution, drafted by coupists, was presented in a public play in an environment where all arguments, interrogations and criticism were banned and those who acted against it were punished. The vote violated the secret voting principle by implementing a transparent envelope. All of this has caused severe damage to the formal legitimacy of the Constitution. Mr. President, the Constitution of 1982, which is rather central to the state than human, has become a continuing function of creating problems, rather than solving social problems, which are significantly restricting basic rights and freedoms. The authoritarian, stateist and guardianship structure of the Constitution, which has lost its internal stability as a result of numerous changes, remains in its word and soul. In order to make our country more manageable in a modern sense, it is time and is about to pass a new constitution based on democracy, civil, liberal and human. We don't have time to lose. It has become impossible to move on with a constitution that is behind the age. This constitution, which has always been an obstacle to progress and social solidarity, must be made a constitution by all of us. A pluralist and liberal constitution, which is based on fundamental rights and freedoms, is the most important expectation of our nation. Experiences in Turkey's recent political history show that it is not possible to build 21st-century Turkey and build 21st-century Turkey with this system established by the 1982 Constitution. The failed coalition period, early elections, instability, and the February 28th postmodern military coup and April 27th review have yielded the consequences of this system's inconsistencies. Mr. President, valuable lawmakers, when we look at the Constitution of 1982 as the final point in Turkish constitutional development, the approach to resolving the periodical issues has completely corrupted the internal balance and cognitive union of the Constitution. For example, it is almost impossible to determine the name of the current government system in the 1982 Constitution. I don't know whether it's Parliamentary or semi-presidential, like a camel or a bird. That is why we want a new and liberal constitution to be built from beginning to end, and with this constitution, new horizons will be opened to our country.
